<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove stable canonical recovery for scalar-twisted group factors of ICC groups commensurable with property-$`(T)`$ lattices over characteristic-zero local fields. Every specified stable isomorphism to a scalar-twisted factor of any countably infinite ICC group recovers the group, the cocycle up to a normalized cochain, and equal amplification scales, with an implementing partial isometry in the given matrix corners. We compute the arithmetic correspondence subcategory for arbitrary countable ICC groups, including all bounded maps, summands, conjugates, and Connes fusion, and combine it with the companion's arithmetic exhaustion theorem. Multiplication also recovers arbitrary finite-index factor neighbors, with the exact projective condition on the matched cocycle ratio.

- **claim-stable-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-stable)): Let $\Lambda\in\mathscr K$ and let $\Pi$ be any countably infinite ICC group. Let $\omega,\nu$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles on them, and let $t,s>0$. Then $$L_\omega(\Lambda)^t\cong L_\nu(\Pi)^s$$ if and only if $t=s$ and there are an isomorphism $\delta:\Lambda\to\Pi$ and a normalized cochain $b:\Lambda\to\mathbb T$ such that $$\label{eq:intro-cochain} \omega(g,h)=b(g)b(h)b(gh)^{-1}\nu(\delta(g),\delta(h)).$$ Every specified isomorphism at unit scale has the form $$\theta(u_g)=w\,b(g)v_{\delta(g)}w^*$$ for a unitary $w$ in the target.* *More explicitly, put $M=L_\omega(\Lambda)$ and $N=L_\nu(\Pi)$, and let $$\theta:pM_n(M)p\longrightarrow qM_m(N)q$$ be a specified isomorphism, where the unnormalized traces of $p$ and $q$ are $t$ and $s$. Then $t=s$, and one may choose $\delta,b$ as above and $z\in M_{m,n}(N)$ so that, for the canonical map $\varphi(u_g)=b(g)v_{\delta(g)}$, $$z^*z=\varphi^{(n)}(p),\qquad zz^*=q, \qquad \theta(x)=z\varphi^{(n)}(x)z^*.$$ Whenever such an isomorphism exists, the recovered comparison group $\Pi$ belongs to $\mathscr K$. In particular every factor $L_\omega(\Lambda)$ in this class has trivial fundamental group.
  - Explanation: For a lattice-class group and any countably infinite ICC comparison group, an isomorphism between amplified twisted factors exists exactly when the scales agree and the groups are isomorphic with the cocycles matching up to a normalized cochain. Every specified isomorphism at unit scale is then a unitary conjugate of the canonical twisted map. The comparison group lies in the class and the fundamental group is trivial.
- **claim-factor-endpoints** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-endpoints)): Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\mu$ be any normalized scalar cocycle on $\Gamma$, and let $P$ be a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor with separable predual. There is a nonzero bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$P$ correspondence if and only if $$P\cong L_\omega(\Lambda)^t$$ for a countably infinite ICC group $\Lambda$, a normalized scalar cocycle $\omega$ on $\Lambda$, and $t>0$, for which there are finite-index subgroups $A\leq\Gamma$ and $B\leq\Lambda$, an actual isomorphism $\delta:A\to B$, and a nonzero finite-dimensional projective unitary representation of $A$ with multiplier $$\frac{\mu\|_{A\times A}}{\delta^*(\omega\|_{B\times B})}.$$ In particular, the reconstructed group $\Lambda$ belongs to $\mathscr K$.
  - Explanation: A separable II_1 factor is joined to a twisted lattice-class group factor by a nonzero bifinite correspondence exactly when it is an amplified twisted group factor of an ICC group whose matched finite-index subgroup data carry a finite-dimensional projective representation of the cocycle ratio. The reconstructed group again belongs to the class.
- **claim-all-correspondences** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-correspondences)): Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\Lambda$ be any countably infinite ICC group, and let $\mu$ and $\omega$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles on these groups.* 1. *Every nonzero bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$L_\omega(\Lambda)$ correspondence is a closed bimodule summand of a finite direct sum of the models in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-model">[eq:intro-model]</a>.* 2. *Every such correspondence has a finite orthogonal decomposition into full-germ orbit bundles. After choosing distinct germ orbits and allowing zero fibres, all bounded bimodule maps are $$\label{eq:intro-hom} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{M\text{--}N}\!\left(\bigoplus_d H(d,\sigma_d), \bigoplus_d H(d,\tau_d)\right) =\bigoplus_d\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{C_d,\alpha}(\sigma_d,\tau_d).$$ Norm, adjoint, and composition are fibrewise. Projection summands are exactly projective-representation summands in the fibres. A correspondence $K=\bigoplus_d H(d,\sigma_d)$ has dimensions $$\dim_M K=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Lambda:B_d],\qquad \dim K_N=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Gamma:A_d].$$ Both are positive integers when $K\ne0$.* 3. *Conjugation and Connes fusion correspond to conjugation and balanced tensor product of these bundles. They are equivalently computed by finite double-coset induction. The scalar transports are given explicitly in Equations <a href="#eq:input-A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A1">[eq:input-A1]</a> and <a href="#eq:input-A2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A2">[eq:input-A2]</a> and Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a>.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero bifinite correspondence is a summand of a finite sum of arithmetic models. Such correspondences split into full-germ orbit bundles whose bounded maps, adjoints, norms and compositions are computed fibrewise. Their two dimensions are explicit positive integers. Conjugation and Connes fusion are computed by bundle conjugation and balanced tensor product.
- **claim-arithmetic-exhaustion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-recovery-exhaustion)): Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\Lambda$ be any countably infinite ICC group, and let $\mu,\omega$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles. Every nonzero separable bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$L_\omega(\Lambda)$ correspondence is a closed bimodule summand of a finite direct sum of the models $E(A,B,\delta,\sigma)$ in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-model">[eq:intro-model]</a>, with the exact multiplier in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-multiplier">[eq:intro-multiplier]</a>.
  - Explanation: This is the geometric input imported from the companion paper: the explicit graph models exhaust all separable bifinite correspondences from a lattice-class source to any ICC group, with the exact multiplier. It is used as a black box.
- **claim-full-stabilizer-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-calculus-germ-test)): The maps $g\mathrel{% \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}% \paperoriginalmapsto \pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(g)]$ and $h\mathrel{% \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}% \paperoriginalmapsto \pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(h)]$ are injective. The group $C_d$ is the graph of an isomorphism $\delta_d:A_d\to B_d$ between finite-index subgroups. It contains $C_\delta$ at finite index, and $\delta_d$ extends $\delta$. If a finite-index subgroup of $C_d$ fixes another germ $x$ from $\Gamma$ to $\Lambda$, then $x=d$. Equivalently, the only finite $C_d$-orbit in the space of these germs is $\{d\}$. The same conclusion holds with $C_d$ replaced by $C_\delta$.
  - Explanation: The full stabilizer of a germ is the graph of an isomorphism between finite-index subgroups containing the presenting graph at finite index. The only finite orbit of the stabilizer on germs is the germ itself, so germ labels are intrinsic.
- **claim-projective-induction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-calculus-induction)): Equation <a href="#eq:input-A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A1">[eq:input-A1]</a> defines a unitary projective representation of $D$ with multiplier $\alpha$. Changing the coset representatives gives a unitarily equivalent bundle by unitary identifications of its individual fibres. If $C$ is the graph of a finite-index subgroup isomorphism $A\to B$, the bundle has commuting normal $M$ and right $N$ actions, and is bifinite.
  - Explanation: The explicit transport formula defines a unitary projective representation of the product group with the joint multiplier. It is independent of coset representatives up to fibrewise unitaries and gives commuting normal bifinite actions when the subgroup is a graph.
- **claim-graph-bundle-unitary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-calculus-graph-model)): Let $\delta:A\to B$ be a finite-index subgroup isomorphism and let $\sigma:A\to\mathcal U(V)$ have multiplier $\beta=\mu\|_{A\times A}/\delta^*\omega$. Define $\gamma(a,\delta(a))=\sigma(a)$. Then $$E(A,B,\delta,\sigma)= L^2(M)\mathbin{\bar\otimes}_{L_{\mu\|A}(A)} (V\otimes L^2(N)) \cong\mathcal H(C_\delta,\gamma).$$ The unitary sends the vector $\xi$ in the fibre labelled by $x$ to $U_{r_x}j\xi$, where $j\xi=\widehat1\otimes(\xi\otimes\widehat1)$ in the displayed model.
  - Explanation: The Connes-product model of a subgroup-isomorphism datum is unitarily the induced graph bundle, with the displayed unitary sending a fibre vector to the translated base vector. This also checks normality of the balanced subgroup action.
- **claim-orbit-dimensions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-calculus-dimensions)): For a finite-dimensional $\alpha\|_{C_d}$-projective representation $\gamma$, $$\label{eq:calculus-full-dimensions} \dim_M H(d,\gamma) =\dim_{\mathbb C}(\gamma)[\Lambda:B_d], \qquad \dim H(d,\gamma)_N =\dim_{\mathbb C}(\gamma)[\Gamma:A_d].$$ For the original model these formulas read $$\dim_M E(A,B,\delta,\sigma) =\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma)[\Lambda:B], \qquad \dim E(A,B,\delta,\sigma)_N =\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma)[\Gamma:A].$$ Zero fibres may be omitted. Dimensions add under finite orthogonal sums.
  - Explanation: The left and right dimensions of a full-germ orbit module are the fibre dimension times the corresponding index of the stabilizer projections; the same formulas hold for the original models with their own subgroups.
- **claim-full-germ-hom** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-calculus-hom)): Choose finitely many distinct $D$-orbits of germs and a representative $d$ in each orbit. Let $\gamma_d,\tau_d$ be finite-dimensional projective representations of $C_d$, with multiplier $\alpha\|_{C_d}$; either may be zero. Then $$\label{eq:calculus-hom} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{M\text{--}N} \left(\bigoplus_dH(d,\gamma_d),\, \bigoplus_dH(d,\tau_d)\right) =\bigoplus_d\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{C_d,\alpha}(\gamma_d,\tau_d).$$ The equality is implemented by restriction to the representative fibres and extension by transport. Norm is the maximum of the fibre norms, and adjoints and composition are fibrewise. In particular all closed bimodule summands are obtained by taking invariant subspaces of these finite-dimensional fibre representations.
  - Explanation: Bounded bimodule maps between sums of full-germ bundles are exactly families of equivariant fibre maps at representative germs, with maximum norm and fibrewise adjoints and composition. Closed bimodule summands correspond to invariant subspaces of the fibre representations.
- **claim-fusion-bundle** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-calculus-fusion)): There is a unitary correspondence identification $$\label{eq:calculus-fusion-unitary} \mathcal H_X\mathbin{\bar\otimes}_{N}\mathcal H_Y \cong\mathcal H_Z$$ under which outer transport is Equation <a href="#eq:input-A2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A2">[eq:input-A2]</a>. The space $Z$ has finitely many outer-group orbits. For each $z=[(x,y)]$, its stabilizer is a finite-index subgroup-isomorphism graph with germ $d_y d_x$. Regrouping these orbits over full stabilizers by Equation <a href="#eq:calculus-regrouping" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:calculus-regrouping">[eq:calculus-regrouping]</a> gives the full-germ decomposition of the fusion.
  - Explanation: Connes fusion of two graph bundles is a counting tensor-fibre bundle over the quotient label space. Its outer transport is the product of the two transports, it has finitely many outer orbits, and each stabilizer is a subgroup-isomorphism graph with the composed germ.
- **claim-extension-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoints-extension)): Let $M,P$ be $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors and let ${}_M K_P\ne0$ be bifinite. The commutant $Q$ of the right $P$-action is a positive finite amplification of $P$, and the given left action identifies $M$ with a unital finite-index subfactor of $Q$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero bifinite correspondence turns the commutant of the right action into a finite amplification of the target factor, and the source factor becomes a finite-index subfactor of it.
- **claim-intrinsic-grading** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoints-grading)): Every bifinite $M$–$M$ correspondence has an orthogonal decomposition $$K=\bigoplus_{d\in D}K_d$$ into finite-dimensional spaces, with only countably many nonzero terms, such that $$u_gK_du_h^*=K_{\iota(g)d\iota(h)^{-1}}.$$ The support is contained in finitely many $\iota(\Gamma)$-double cosets. For every representative $\delta:A\to B$ of $d$, $$K_d=\left\{\xi\in K: \dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{span} \{u_{\delta(a)}\xi u_a^*:a\in A\}<\infty\right\}. \label{eq:input-B1}$$ In particular, for the fixed presentation $M=L_\mu(\Gamma)$, the grading is determined by the two actions and does not depend on a choice of arithmetic model decomposition.
  - Explanation: A bifinite self-correspondence of the twisted group factor decomposes orthogonally into finite-dimensional pieces indexed by virtual automorphism germs, covariantly for the group unitaries, and each piece is characterised intrinsically by finite-dimensionality of orbit spans.
- **claim-bounded-grades** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoints-bounded-grades)): Every $K_d$ consists of bounded elements of $Q$, and $$K_dK_f\subset K_{df},\qquad K_d^*=K_{d^{-1}},\qquad 1\in K_1,\qquad u_a\in K_{\iota(a)}. \label{eq:endpoints-grade-product}$$ Consequently $K_1$ is a finite-dimensional unital $*$-subalgebra of $Q$, normalized by the $u_a$, and $\tau_Q(K_d)=0$ for $d\ne1$.
  - Explanation: Each graded piece consists of bounded operators, the grading is multiplicative and respects adjoints, and the identity piece is a finite-dimensional unital subalgebra normalized by the group unitaries. The trace vanishes on other degrees.
- **claim-minimal-corner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoints-minimal-corner)): Let $p$ be a minimal projection of $K_1$. There are a countable subgroup $\Lambda\leq D$ and a normalized scalar cocycle $\omega$ such that the actual corner $pQp$, with normalized trace, is isomorphic to $L_\omega(\Lambda)$. Each nonzero space $pK_dp$ is one-dimensional and has a unitary basis vector in the corner.
  - Explanation: Cutting down by a minimal projection of the identity piece gives a corner isomorphic to a twisted group factor of a countable group of germs, with each nonzero graded piece of the corner a unitary line.
- **claim-corner-constraints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoints-corner-constraints)): For the corner in Lemma <a href="#lem:endpoints-minimal-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:endpoints-minimal-corner">16</a>, the group $\Lambda$ is countably infinite and ICC. There are finite-index subgroups $A\leq\Gamma$, $B\leq\Lambda$, an actual group isomorphism $\delta:A\to B$, and a finite-dimensional projective representation with multiplier $\mu\|_A/\delta^*(\omega\|_B)$.
  - Explanation: The group from the minimal corner is countably infinite and ICC, contains a finite-index copy of a finite-index subgroup of the original group, and the identity block supplies a finite-dimensional projective representation of the exact cocycle ratio.
- **claim-equivalence-dimensions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoints-equivalence-dimensions)): For composable bifinite correspondences of finite factors, left dimensions multiply under relative tensor product, and so do right dimensions. If $M^t=p(M_n(\mathbb C)\bar\otimes M)p$ and $C_p=pL^2(M)^n$, then ${}_{M^t}C_p{}_M$ is an equivalence correspondence with dimensions $(1/t,t)$ in left/right order.
  - Explanation: Dimensions are multiplicative under relative tensor product on both sides, and a matrix corner column gives an equivalence correspondence whose two dimensions are reciprocal.
- **claim-commensurability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-commensurability)): Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$ and let $\Lambda$ be any countably infinite ICC group. There is a nonzero bifinite $L(\Gamma)$–$L(\Lambda)$ correspondence if and only if $\Gamma$ and $\Lambda$ are abstractly commensurable.
  - Explanation: For a lattice-class group and an arbitrary countably infinite ICC group, a nonzero bifinite correspondence between the untwisted factors exists exactly when the groups are abstractly commensurable.
- **claim-untwisted-neighbors** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-untwisted-neighbors)): For $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, a separable $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $P$ is joined to $L(\Gamma)$ by a nonzero bifinite correspondence if and only if $P\cong L_\omega(\Lambda)^t$ for $t>0$, an ICC group $\Lambda$ abstractly commensurable with $\Gamma$, and a finite-type cocycle $\omega$ on $\Lambda$.
  - Explanation: The factor neighbors of an untwisted lattice-class group factor are the amplified twisted factors of commensurable ICC groups with finite-type cocycles.
- **claim-outer-automorphisms** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-outer-automorphisms)): For $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, there is an isomorphism of abstract groups $$\operatorname{Out}(L(\Gamma)) \cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\Gamma,\mathbb T)\rtimes\operatorname{Out}(\Gamma), \qquad [\delta]\cdot\chi=\chi\circ\delta^{-1},$$ where $\operatorname{Out}$ denotes automorphisms modulo inner automorphisms.
  - Explanation: The outer automorphism group of the group factor is the semidirect product of the character group with the outer automorphism group of the group.
- **claim-reduced-cstar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-reduced-cstar)): Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$ and suppose that $C_r^*(\Gamma)$ has a unique tracial state. If $\Lambda$ is any discrete group and there is a complex-linear $*$-isomorphism $C_r^*(\Gamma)\cong C_r^*(\Lambda)$, then $\Gamma\cong\Lambda$.
  - Explanation: When the reduced C-star algebra of a lattice-class group has a unique tracial state, any isomorphism with the reduced algebra of a discrete group forces the groups to be isomorphic.
- **claim-sl3-covolume** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sl3-covolume)): An injective homomorphism $\alpha:\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z)\to\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z)$ whose image has finite index is surjective.
  - Explanation: An injective endomorphism of the integral special linear group of finite index image is onto, by strong rigidity and preservation of covolume.
- **claim-sl3-endomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-sl3-endomorphism)): Every normal unital complex-linear $*$-endomorphism $\theta$ of $M=L(\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z))$ with $[M:\theta(M)]<\infty$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Every finite-index unital normal endomorphism of the group factor of the integral special linear group is surjective, combining integer dimensions with the covolume lemma.

        "exact_quote": "The maps $g\\mathrel{%\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}%\n  \\paperoriginalmapsto\n  \\pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(g)]$ and $h\\mathrel{%\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}%\n  \\paperoriginalmapsto\n  \\pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(h)]$ are injective. The group $C_d$ is the graph of an isomorphism $\\delta_d:A_d\\to B_d$ between finite-index subgroups. It contains $C_\\delta$ at finite index, and $\\delta_d$ extends $\\delta$. If a finite-index subgroup of $C_d$ fixes another germ $x$ from $\\Gamma$ to $\\Lambda$, then $x=d$. Equivalently, the only finite $C_d$-orbit in the space of these germs is $\\{d\\}$. The same conclusion holds with $C_d$ replaced by $C_\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-286.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-calculus-induction",
        "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#eq:input-A1\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:input-A1\">[eq:input-A1]</a> defines a unitary projective representation of $D$ with multiplier $\\alpha$. Changing the coset representatives gives a unitarily equivalent bundle by unitary identifications of its individual fibres.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-calculus-graph-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\delta:A\\to B$ be a finite-index subgroup isomorphism and let $\\sigma:A\\to\\mathcal U(V)$ have multiplier $\\beta=\\mu|_{A\\times A}/\\delta^*\\omega$. Define $\\gamma(a,\\delta(a))=\\sigma(a)$. Then $$E(A,B,\\delta,\\sigma)=\n L^2(M)\\mathbin{\\bar\\otimes}_{L_{\\mu|A}(A)}\n       (V\\otimes L^2(N))\n \\cong\\mathcal H(C_\\delta,\\gamma).$$ The unitary sends the vector $\\xi$ in the fibre labelled by $x$ to $U_{r_x}j\\xi$, where $j\\xi=\\widehat1\\otimes(\\xi\\otimes\\widehat1)$ in the displayed model.",
        "source_id": "PM-286.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-calculus-graph-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\delta:A\\to B$ be a finite-index subgroup isomorphism and let $\\sigma:A\\to\\mathcal U(V)$ have multiplier $\\beta=\\mu|_{A\\times A}/\\delta^*\\omega$.           "source_id": "PM-286.2"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-calculus-dimensions",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite-dimensional $\\alpha|_{C_d}$-projective representation $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:calculus-full-dimensions}\n \\dim_M H(d,\\gamma)\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\gamma)[\\Lambda:B_d],\n \\qquad\n \\dim H(d,\\gamma)_N\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\gamma)[\\Gamma:A_d].$$ For the original model these formulas read $$\\dim_M E(A,B,\\delta,\\sigma)\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\sigma)[\\Lambda:B],\n \\qquad\n \\dim E(A,B,\\delta,\\sigma)_N\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\sigma)[\\Gamma:A].$$ Zero fibres may be omitted. Dimensions add under finite orthogonal sums.",
        "source_id": "PM-286.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-calculus-dimensions",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite-dimensional $\\alpha|_{C_d}$-projective representation $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:calculus-full-dimensions}\n \\dim_M H(d,\\gamma)\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\gamma)[\\Lambda:B_d],\n \\qquad\n \\dim H(d,\\gamma)_N\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\gamma)[\\Gamma:A_d].$$ For the original model these formulas read $$\\dim_M E(A,B,\\delta,\\sigma)\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\sigma)[\\Lambda:B],\n \\qquad\n \\dim E(A,B,\\delta,\\sigma)_N\n   =\\dim_{\\mathbb C}(\\sigma)[\\Gamma:A].$$ Zero fibres may be omitted. Dimensions add under finite orthogonal sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-286.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-calculus-hom",
        "exact_quote": "Choose finitely many distinct $D$-orbits of germs and a representative $d$ in each orbit. Let $\\gamma_d,\\tau_d$ be finite-dimensional projective representations of $C_d$, with multiplier $\\alpha|_{C_d}$; either may be zero. Then $$\\label{eq:calculus-hom}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{M\\text{--}N}\n \\left(\\bigoplus_dH(d,\\gamma_d),\\,\n       \\bigoplus_dH(d,\\tau_d)\\right)\n =\\bigoplus_d\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{C_d,\\alpha}(\\gamma_d,\\tau_d).$$ The equality is implemented by restriction to the representative fibres and extension by transport. Norm is the maximum of the fibre norms, and adjoints and composition are fibrewise. In particular all closed bimodule summands are obtained by taking invariant subspaces of these finite-dimensional fibre representations.",
        "source_id": "PM-286.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-calculus-hom",
          "exact_quote": "Choose finitely many distinct $D$-orbits of germs and a representative $d$ in each orbit. Norm is the maximum of the fibre norms, and adjoints and composition are fibrewise. In particular all closed bimodule summands are obtained by taking invariant subspaces of these finite-dimensional fibre representations.",
          "source_id": "PM-286.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Choose finitely many distinct $D$-orbits of germs and a representative $d$ in each orbit. Norm is the maximum of the fibre norms, and adjoints and composition are fibrewise. In particular all closed bimodule summands are obtained by taking invariant subspaces of these finite-dimensional fibre representations.",
      "explanation": "Bounded bimodule maps between sums of full-germ bundles are exactly families of equivariant fibre maps at representative germs, with maximum norm and fibrewise adjoints and composition. Closed bimodule summands correspond to invariant subspaces of the fibre representations.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-calculus-fusion",
        "exact_quote": "There is a unitary correspondence identification $$\\label{eq:calculus-fusion-unitary}\n \\mathcal H_X\\mathbin{\\bar\\otimes}_{N}\\mathcal H_Y\n \\cong\\mathcal H_Z$$ under which outer transport is Equation <a href=\"#eq:input-A2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:input-A2\">[eq:input-A2]</a>. The space $Z$ has finitely many outer-group orbits. For each $z=[(x,y)]$, its stabilizer is a finite-index subgroup-isomorphism graph with germ $d_y d_x$. Regrouping these orbits over full stabilizers by Equation <a href=\"#eq:calculus-regrouping\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:calculus-regrouping\">[eq:calculus-regrouping]</a> gives the full-germ decomposition of the fusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-286.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-calculus-fusion",
          "exact_quote": "There is a unitary correspondence identification $$\\label{eq:calculus-fusion-unitary}\n \\mathcal H_X\\mathbin{\\bar\\otimes}_{N}\\mathcal H_Y\n \\cong\\mathcal H_Z$$ under which outer transport is Equation <a href=\"#eq:input-A2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:input-A2\">[eq:input-A2]</a>. The space $Z$ has finitely many outer-group orbits. For each $z=[(x,y)]$, its stabilizer is a finite-index subgroup-isomorphism graph with germ $d_y d_x$. Regrouping these orbits over full stabilizers by Equation <a href=\"#eq:calculus-regrouping\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:calculus-regrouping\">[eq:calculus-regrouping]</a> gives the full-germ decomposition of the fusion.",
          "source_id": "PM-286.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a unitary correspondence identification $$\\label{eq:calculus-fusion-unitary}\n \\mathcal H_X\\mathbin{\\bar\\otimes}_{N}\\mathcal H_Y\n \\cong\\mathcal H_Z$$ under which outer transport is Equation <a href=\"#eq:input-A2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:input-A2\">[eq:input-A2]</a>. The space $Z$ has finitely many outer-group orbits. For each $z=[(x,y)]$, its stabilizer is a finite-index subgroup-isomorphism graph with germ $d_y d_x$. Regrouping these orbits over full stabilizers by Equation <a href=\"#eq:calculus-regrouping\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:calculus-regrouping\">[eq:calculus-regrouping]</a> gives the full-germ decomposition of the fusion.",
      "explanation": "Connes fusion of two graph bundles is a counting tensor-fibre bundle over the quotient label space. Its outer transport is the product of the two transports, it has finitely many outer orbits, and each stabilizer is a subgroup-isomorphism graph with the composed germ.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $M,P$ be $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors and let ${}_M K_P\\ne0$ be bifinite. The commutant $Q$ of the right $P$-action is a positive finite amplification of $P$, and the given left action identifies $M$ with a unital finite-index subfactor of $Q$.",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-endpoints-grading",
        "exact_quote": "Every bifinite $M$–$M$ correspondence has an orthogonal decomposition $$K=\\bigoplus_{d\\in D}K_d$$ into finite-dimensional spaces, with only countably many nonzero terms, such that $$u_gK_du_h^*=K_{\\iota(g)d\\iota(h)^{-1}}.$$ The support is contained in finitely many $\\iota(\\Gamma)$-double cosets. For every representative $\\delta:A\\to B$ of $d$, $$K_d=\\left\\{\\xi\\in K:\n  \\dim_{\\mathbb C}\\operatorname{span}\n    \\{u_{\\delta(a)}\\xi u_a^*:a\\in A\\}<\\infty\\right\\}.\n \\label{eq:input-B1}$$ In particular, for the fixed presentation $M=L_\\mu(\\Gamma)$, the grading is determined by the two actions and does not depend on a choice of arithmetic model decomposition.",

      },
      "evidence": [

      "exact_statement": "Every bifinite $M$–$M$ correspondence has an orthogonal decomposition $$K=\\bigoplus_{d\\in D}K_d$$ into finite-dimensional spaces, with only countably many nonzero terms, such that $$u_gK_du_h^*=K_{\\iota(g)d\\iota(h)^{-1}}.$$ The support is contained in finitely many $\\iota(\\Gamma)$-double cosets. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **class of lattice-type ICC groups** ([source](#stmt-def-class)): Let $\mathscr K$ be the class of countably infinite ICC groups abstractly commensurable with a lattice in a finite nonempty product $$G=\prod_{i=1}^r G_i,\qquad G_i=\mathbf H_i(k_i)^+.$$ Here $k_i$ is $\mathbb R$, $\mathbb C$, or a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_p$; the primes may differ. The algebraic group $\mathbf H_i$ is connected, adjoint, and absolutely simple over $k_i$. The superscript $+$ denotes the subgroup generated by the $k_i$-points of the unipotent radicals of proper $k_i$-parabolic subgroups. Each $G_i$ is required to be noncompact and to have Kazhdan’s property $(T)$. A lattice is discrete and has finite Haar covolume. Abstract commensurability means an actual isomorphism between finite-index subgroups. ICC means that every nonidentity conjugacy class is infinite. No irreducibility, cocompactness, product decomposition, or torsion hypothesis is imposed on the lattice.
- **lattice, commensurability, ICC** ([source](#stmt-def-class)): A lattice is discrete and has finite Haar covolume.
- **abstract commensurability** ([source](#stmt-def-class)): Abstract commensurability means an actual isomorphism between finite-index subgroups.
- **ICC** ([source](#stmt-def-class)): ICC means that every nonidentity conjugacy class is infinite.
- **property (T)** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Property $(T)$ means that every continuous unitary representation with almost invariant unit vectors has a nonzero invariant vector.
- **twisted group factor** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The twisted group factor $L_\mu(\Gamma)$ has canonical unitaries $u_g$ with $u_gu_h=\mu(g,h)u_{gh}$ and trace $\tau(u_g)=\mathbf1_{\{e\}}(g)$.
- **bifinite correspondence** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A bifinite $M$–$N$ correspondence is a Hilbert space with commuting normal unital left $M$ and right $N$ actions and finite von Neumann module dimensions on both sides.
- **fundamental group** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The fundamental group of $Q$ is $\mathcal F(Q)=\{t>0:Q^t\cong Q\}$.
- **finite-index factor neighbor** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *finite-index factor neighbor* of $M$ is a factor joined to $M$ by a nonzero bifinite correspondence.
- **projective unitary representation with multiplier** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a group $A$, a normalized projective unitary representation with multiplier $\beta$ is a map $\sigma:A\to\mathcal U(V)$ satisfying $\sigma(a)\sigma(b)=\beta(a,b)\sigma(ab)$ and $\sigma(e)=I$.
- **finite type multiplier** ([source](#pm-h-5)): We call $\beta$ *finite type* when such a representation exists with $0<\dim_{\mathbb C}V<\infty$; its image need not be finite.
- **pullback cocycle** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a group homomorphism $\delta$, the pullback cocycle is $\delta^*\omega(a,b)=\omega(\delta(a),\delta(b))$.
- **virtual-isomorphism germ** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *virtual-isomorphism germ* from $\Gamma$ to $\Lambda$ is an isomorphism between finite-index subgroups, modulo agreement on a common finite-index subgroup.
- **full stabilizer of a germ** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a germ $d$ let $C_d$ denote its full stabilizer.
- **right bounded vector** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A vector $\xi$ in an $M$–$N$ correspondence is *right bounded* if $R_\xi\widehat a=\xi a$ extends to a bounded map from $L^2(N)$.
- **graph of a representative** ([source](#pm-h-13)): If $\delta:A\to B$ represents $d$, denote its graph by $C_\delta=\{(a,\delta(a)):a\in A\}$.
- **induced bundle space** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Set $\mathcal H(C,\gamma)=\bigoplus_{x\in X}V_x$, with the counting Hilbert norm.
- **reduced group C-star algebra** ([source](#pm-h-27)): Write $C_r^*(\Gamma)$ for the operator-norm closure of the group algebra in the left regular representation.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-scale-equality | equality of the two amplification scales | = | $t=s$ | Positive scales for which an isomorphism of amplifications exists. |
| q-fundamental-group | fundamental group of twisted lattice-class factors | = | $\{1\}$ | All normalized scalar cocycles. |
| q-left-dimension | left dimension of a bifinite correspondence as a sum over germ orbits | = | $\dim_M K=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Lambda:B_d]$ | Fibre dimensions and indices of the stabilizer projections. |
| q-right-dimension | right dimension of a bifinite correspondence as a sum over germ orbits | = | $\dim K_N=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Gamma:A_d]$ | Fibre dimensions and indices of the stabilizer projections. |
| q-corner-column-dimensions | left and right dimensions of a corner column correspondence | = | $(1/t,t)$ | Positive scale t of the corner. |
| q-equivalence-left-dimension | left dimension of the equivalence correspondence of a specified stable isomorphism | = | $t/s$ | Scales before integrality forces them equal. |
| q-left-dimension-over-commutant | dimension of the correspondence as a left module over the commutant | = | $1/r$ | r is the right dimension. |
| q-sup-norm-bound | operator norm of a homogeneous element against its two-norm | ≤ | $[Q:M]^{1/2}\\|\xi\\|_2$ | Vectors in a graded piece. |
| q-trace-distance | trace two-norm distance between distinct canonical group unitaries | = | $\sqrt2$ | Distinct group elements. |
| q-sl3-index | index of a finite-index injective endomorphism image of the integral special linear group | = | $1$ | Injective endomorphisms of finite-index image. |
| q-endomorphism-index | left dimension of the endomorphism correspondence | = | $[M:\theta(M)]$ | Normal unital endomorphisms. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-germ-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-calculus-germ-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-calculus-germ-test): Trivial centralizers of finite-index subgroups of ICC groups give injectivity of inner germ maps, so stabilizers are graphs; a finite orbit test shows agreement on a finite-index subgroup. -> Full stabilizer is a finite-index graph and the unique finite orbit is the germ.
- **step-induction** [claim](#stmt-lem-calculus-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-calculus-induction): Cocycle identity for the correction terms gives the transport identity; freeness of the coordinate actions yields regular modules. -> Unitary projective induction with bifinite normal actions.
- **step-graph-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-calculus-graph-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-calculus-graph-model): Check normality of the balanced subgroup action, decompose the relative product over cosets, and match the transport with the induction formula. -> Connes-product model is unitarily the graph bundle.
- **step-dimensions** [claim](#stmt-prop-calculus-dimensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-calculus-dimensions): Count copies of the standard left and right modules from the free coordinate orbits. -> Orbit module dimensions as fibre dimension times index.
- **step-full-germ-hom** [claim](#stmt-thm-calculus-hom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-calculus-hom): Square summability of columns plus constancy of block norm along stabilizer orbits and the finite-orbit test force diagonality; extend fibre intertwiners by transport. -> Hom formula and description of summands by fibre projections.
- **step-fusion** [claim](#stmt-prop-calculus-fusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-calculus-fusion): Use orthonormal free right bases to identify the relative product with the counting tensor bundle, then compute stabilizers by double cosets. -> Fusion as a tensor-fibre bundle with composed germs.
- **step-exhaustion-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-recovery-exhaustion) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Imported from the companion paper as the geometric input; not reproved here. -> Every bifinite correspondence is a summand of a sum of models.
- **step-all-correspondences** [claim](#stmt-thm-correspondences) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Combine exhaustion with the full-germ calculus, conjugation and fusion formulas. -> Classification of all finite correspondences with dimensions, conjugates and fusion.
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoints-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-extension): Column realization of the right module, comparison of module projections and Jones index finiteness. -> Finite-index extension of the source factor inside an amplification of the target.
- **step-grading** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoints-grading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-grading): Apply the correspondence classification with inverted germ labels; characterise degree spaces by finite orbit spans using a mass argument. -> Intrinsic grading by virtual automorphism germs.
- **step-bounded-grades** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoints-bounded-grades) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-bounded-grades): Orthogonality of degrees and the Pimsner-Popa inequality bound homogeneous vectors; products are checked through the orbit-span test. -> Bounded graded pieces with multiplicative grading.
- **step-minimal-corner** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoints-minimal-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-minimal-corner): Compress to a minimal projection so each surviving degree is a unitary line, define the group and cocycle, and show the generated algebra fills the corner. -> Corner isomorphic to a twisted group factor.
- **step-corner-constraints** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoints-corner-constraints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoints-corner-constraints): Split the action on the central block with matrix implementers, compare multipliers to obtain the ratio, then use commensuration to get finite index and ICC. -> ICC group with a matched finite-index subgroup and exact ratio representation.
- **step-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-thm-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoints-corner-constraints-2): Apply the extension, grading and corner results; the converse composes the arithmetic correspondence with a corner equivalence and the given isomorphism. -> All factor endpoints are amplified twisted group factors with the ratio condition.
- **step-equivalence-dimensions** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoints-equivalence-dimensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-equivalence-dimensions): Realize modules as columns and compute the trace of the projection; conjugate for the other side. -> Multiplicative dimensions and reciprocal corner column dimensions.
- **step-stable-recovery** [claim](#stmt-thm-stable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoints-equivalence-dimensions-2): Form the equivalence correspondence of the specified isomorphism, use integrality of dimensions to force one orbit and equal scales, read off the isomorphism and cochain, then recover the partial isometry. -> Equal scales, group isomorphism, cochain and implementing partial isometry.
- **step-commensurability** [claim](#stmt-cor-commensurability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-commensurability): A nonzero correspondence has a nonzero full-germ orbit whose stabilizer is a graph; conversely use the trivial model. -> Correspondence existence is equivalent to commensurability.
- **step-untwisted-neighbors** [claim](#stmt-cor-untwisted-neighbors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-untwisted-neighbors): Necessity from the endpoint theorem with projective induction; sufficiency by restricting and conjugating the representation. -> Description of untwisted factor neighbors.
- **step-outer-automorphisms** [claim](#stmt-cor-outer-automorphisms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-outer-automorphisms): Define the homomorphism from characters and group automorphisms, get surjectivity from the unit-scale recovery and injectivity from a Fourier orbit argument and ICC. -> Out of the group factor as a semidirect product.
- **step-reduced-cstar** [claim](#stmt-cor-reduced-cstar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-reduced-cstar): Unique trace extends the isomorphism to the group factors, then apply stable recovery at trivial cocycles and unit scales. -> Isomorphic groups.
- **step-sl3-covolume** [claim](#stmt-lem-sl3-covolume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sl3-covolume): Strong rigidity extends the isomorphism to a Lie group automorphism preserving Haar measure, so covolumes match and the index is one. -> Finite-index injective endomorphism is surjective.
- **step-sl3-endomorphism** [claim](#stmt-cor-sl3-endomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-sl3-endomorphism): Injectivity from factoriality and trace; the endomorphism correspondence has right dimension one so a single graph orbit appears; apply the covolume lemma and the left dimension formula. -> Finite-index endomorphism is onto.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-stable-recovery**: For a source group in the lattice class and an arbitrary countably infinite ICC comparison group, an isomorphism between amplified twisted group factors forces equal amplification scales and a group isomorphism whose cocycle ratio is a normalized coboundary.
- **inv-integer-dimensions**: Every nonzero bifinite correspondence between the twisted group factors has left and right dimensions that are positive integers, computed fibrewise from projective representation dimensions and subgroup indices.
- **inv-endpoint-ratio**: A separable II_1 factor is joined to a twisted lattice group factor by a nonzero bifinite correspondence exactly when it is an amplified twisted group factor of an ICC group with a finite-dimensional projective representation of the matched cocycle ratio.
- **inv-dimension-multiplicativity**: Left dimensions multiply under relative tensor product of composable bifinite correspondences of finite factors, and so do right dimensions.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-property-t-alone**: Conclude that two ICC groups with Kazhdan's property (T) and isomorphic group factors must be isomorphic. -> Recovery fails for property (T) groups in general; pairwise nonisomorphic ICC property (T) groups with isomorphic group factors have been constructed.
- **anti-decision-procedure**: Use the classification as a decision procedure for commensurability or factor isomorphism of groups given by presentations. -> The results are structural classifications and give no algorithm; the proof relies on an external exhaustion theorem.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are structural classification and recovery theorems that depend on an external geometric exhaustion theorem over infinite groups and infinite-dimensional factors; the paper explicitly states that they do not assert a decision procedure, so there is no finite constructive procedure to implement and certify.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is a structural classification of correspondences between twisted group factors. It supplies no program, interface or data format that a software component could implement, and the geometric input is imported rather than computed. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the statements concerns state evolution, physical dynamics or procedural generation. The objects are infinite operator algebras with no simulation semantics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fibrewise formulas could in principle guide a symbolic helper, but the source gives no concrete procedure, input format or termination guarantee, so no tool contract can be stated honestly. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results are existence and classification statements whose proofs depend on an external exhaustion theorem. No finite procedure with stated inputs, outputs and cost is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fibre bundles over germ orbits and cosets are proof devices on infinite groups. The source specifies no finite representation, indexing scheme or operation costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, dataflow or parallel structure is described. The integer dimensions are outputs of a theorem, not of a computation that hardware could accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results give no hardness assumption, reduction or primitive. Recovering a group from its factor is a rigidity theorem about operator algebras, not a security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, estimator or learning guarantee. Cocycle twists and amplification scales are algebraic parameters, not model parameters. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimality statement appears. The forced equality of amplification scales is an integrality consequence, not the outcome of an optimisation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages, faults or consensus. Finite-index subgroups and bimodules carry no protocol meaning. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is pure operator algebra: rigidity, fundamental groups and finite-index neighbors of group factors. This is mathematical consequence, not an engineering application with a construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-lattice-class-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The recovery and classification theorems are proved only when the source group lies in the stated class of countably infinite ICC groups commensurable with lattices in products of adjoint simple local-field groups with property (T). Property (T) alone does not give recovery. Blocked use: ['Applying stable canonical recovery to an arbitrary ICC property (T) group outside the lattice class', 'Concluding group isomorphism from isomorphic group factors without checking class membership']
- **limit-icc-countable-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The comparison group must be countably infinite and ICC; the ICC property is what makes the twisted group algebra a factor and makes germ stabilizers graphs of finite-index subgroup isomorphisms. Blocked use: ['Using the germ calculus for abelian, solvable or otherwise non-ICC groups', 'Using uncountable comparison groups']
- **limit-external-exhaustion-input** (nonconstructive): The classification of all correspondences relies on the arithmetic exhaustion statement imported from the companion paper and used as a black box; it is not reproved here and gives no procedure. Blocked use: ['Treating the full correspondence classification as self-contained or effective', 'Claiming an algorithm that produces the summand decomposition of a given correspondence']
- **limit-no-decision-procedure** (formal_scope): The results are structural classifications and do not assert a decision procedure for arbitrary group presentations or cocycle classes. Blocked use: ['Using the theorems as a decision procedure for commensurability of groups given by presentations', 'Quoting running times or complexity for the classification']
- **limit-free-group-contrast** (counterexample_scope): Outside the property (T) setting rigidity fails: nonisomorphic free groups have isomorphic group factors, and their fundamental group is not trivial. Blocked use: ['Transferring trivial fundamental group or group recovery to free group factors']
- **limit-ratio-condition-endpoints** (hypothesis): For arbitrary source twist only the matched cocycle ratio is required to have a finite-dimensional projective representation; finite type of an individual cocycle is obtained only when the source twist is trivial. Blocked use: ['Assuming each endpoint cocycle is finite type for general twisted neighbors']
- **limit-sl3-covolume-scope** (formal_scope): The surjectivity statement for endomorphisms uses a finite-index image hypothesis and a classical covolume argument specific to the integral special linear group of the stated size. Blocked use: ['Dropping the finite-index hypothesis on the endomorphism', 'Applying the covolume argument to other groups without checking strong rigidity applies']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove stable canonical recovery for scalar-twisted group factors of ICC groups commensurable with property-$`(T)`$ lattices over characteristic-zero local fields. Every specified stable isomorphism to a scalar-twisted factor of any countably infinite ICC group recovers the group, the cocycle up to a normalized cochain, and equal amplification scales, with an implementing partial isometry in the given matrix corners. We compute the arithmetic correspondence subcategory for arbitrary countable ICC groups, including all bounded maps, summands, conjugates, and Connes fusion, and combine it with the companion's arithmetic exhaustion theorem. Multiplication also recovers arbitrary finite-index factor neighbors, with the exact projective condition on the matched cocycle ratio.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A group factor is built from the multiplication of a discrete group, but an isomorphism of factors need not preserve its distinguished group unitaries. We ask when the group, a scalar twist of its multiplication, and the given factor isomorphism can all be recovered. For the lattice class below, we prove this recovery even after amplification and with an arbitrary ICC comparison group.

Connes proved countability of the fundamental group of a property-$(T)$ group factor (Connes 1980) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Connes1980](#ref-Connes1980)]; his later problems ask whether nonisomorphic ICC property-$(T)$ groups have nonisomorphic factors, and ask for the fundamental groups of those factors (Connes 1994, chap. V, Appendix B.$\varepsilon$, Problems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Connes1994](#ref-Connes1994)]. The stronger stable formulation displayed by Popa asks for recovery of the given isomorphism up to a character and inner conjugacy, with amplification scale one (Popa 2007, sec. 3, Equations (3.2) and (3.2$'$)) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Popa2007](#ref-Popa2007)].

The proof uses the more general relation of finite correspondence: two factors act normally and commute on one Hilbert space, with finite module dimensions on both sides. We determine these correspondences through isomorphisms between finite-index subgroups and finite-dimensional projective representations. The representation records the ratio of the two scalar cocycles on the matched subgroups. Neither cocycle separately need have a finite-dimensional realization.

Our algebraic calculation identifies every bounded map, summand, and composition of these arithmetic models for arbitrary countable ICC groups. The geometric exhaustion theorem of the companion (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ArithmeticCompanion](#ref-ArithmeticCompanion)] makes the models exhaustive for the lattice class. Their integral dimensions recover stable isomorphisms; their compatibility with multiplication also recovers arbitrary finite-index factor neighbors.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The common conventions and lattice class

<a id="stmt-def-class"></a>
<div id="def:class" class="definition">

**Definition 1**. Let $\mathscr K$ be the class of countably infinite ICC groups abstractly commensurable with a lattice in a finite nonempty product $$G=\prod_{i=1}^r G_i,\qquad G_i=\mathbf H_i(k_i)^+.$$ Here $k_i$ is $\mathbb R$, $\mathbb C$, or a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_p$; the primes may differ. The algebraic group $\mathbf H_i$ is connected, adjoint, and absolutely simple over $k_i$. The superscript $+$ denotes the subgroup generated by the $k_i$-points of the unipotent radicals of proper $k_i$-parabolic subgroups. Each $G_i$ is required to be noncompact and to have Kazhdan’s property $(T)$.

A lattice is discrete and has finite Haar covolume. Abstract commensurability means an actual isomorphism between finite-index subgroups. ICC means that every nonidentity conjugacy class is infinite. No irreducibility, cocompactness, product decomposition, or torsion hypothesis is imposed on the lattice.

</div>

For example, $\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z)$ and every finite-index subgroup belong to $\mathscr K$. Section <a href="#sec:examples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:examples">5</a> verifies the lattice and ICC hypotheses for these and other named examples.

Property $(T)$ means that every continuous unitary representation with almost invariant unit vectors has a nonzero invariant vector. Standard structure and permanence results for this class are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a>; see also (Bekka, Harpe, and Valette 2008) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BHV2008](#ref-BHV2008)].

All scalar cocycles take values in $\mathbb T$ and are normalized: $$\mu(g,h)\mu(gh,j)=\mu(h,j)\mu(g,hj),\qquad
 \mu(e,g)=\mu(g,e)=1.$$ The twisted group factor $L_\mu(\Gamma)$ has canonical unitaries $u_g$ with $u_gu_h=\mu(g,h)u_{gh}$ and trace $\tau(u_g)=\mathbf1_{\{e\}}(g)$. For an ICC group it is a factor; Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a> recalls the short Fourier argument.

A bifinite $M$–$N$ correspondence is a Hilbert space with commuting normal unital left $M$ and right $N$ actions and finite von Neumann module dimensions on both sides. The actions need not be mutual commutants, and the correspondence need not be irreducible. Every correspondence in this paper is separable; every factor isomorphism is normal, unital, complex-linear, and $*$-preserving. For a finite factor $Q$, write $$Q^t=p\bigl(M_n(\mathbb C)\,\overline\otimes\,Q\bigr)p,
 \qquad (\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_n\otimes\tau_Q)(p)=t>0.$$ The matrix trace $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_n$ is unnormalized; the corner itself has its normalized trace. The fundamental group of $Q$ is $\mathcal F(Q)=\{t>0:Q^t\cong Q\}$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Stable canonical recovery

A stable comparison is an isomorphism between positive amplifications of two factors. The following theorem forces the amplification scales to agree and determines the given map on its actual matrix corners.

<a id="stmt-thm-stable"></a>
<div id="thm:stable" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:intro-cochain"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Presentation equivalence and stable canonical recovery). *Let $\Lambda\in\mathscr K$ and let $\Pi$ be any countably infinite ICC group. Let $\omega,\nu$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles on them, and let $t,s>0$. Then $$L_\omega(\Lambda)^t\cong L_\nu(\Pi)^s$$ if and only if $t=s$ and there are an isomorphism $\delta:\Lambda\to\Pi$ and a normalized cochain $b:\Lambda\to\mathbb T$ such that $$\label{eq:intro-cochain}
 \omega(g,h)=b(g)b(h)b(gh)^{-1}\nu(\delta(g),\delta(h)).$$ Every specified isomorphism at unit scale has the form $$\theta(u_g)=w\,b(g)v_{\delta(g)}w^*$$ for a unitary $w$ in the target.*

*More explicitly, put $M=L_\omega(\Lambda)$ and $N=L_\nu(\Pi)$, and let $$\theta:pM_n(M)p\longrightarrow qM_m(N)q$$ be a specified isomorphism, where the unnormalized traces of $p$ and $q$ are $t$ and $s$. Then $t=s$, and one may choose $\delta,b$ as above and $z\in M_{m,n}(N)$ so that, for the canonical map $\varphi(u_g)=b(g)v_{\delta(g)}$, $$z^*z=\varphi^{(n)}(p),\qquad zz^*=q,
 \qquad \theta(x)=z\varphi^{(n)}(x)z^*.$$ Whenever such an isomorphism exists, the recovered comparison group $\Pi$ belongs to $\mathscr K$. In particular every factor $L_\omega(\Lambda)$ in this class has trivial fundamental group.*

</div>

The untwisted specialization answers the stable recovery question for Definition <a href="#def:class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:class">1</a>, with an arbitrary ICC comparison group. Property $(T)$ alone does not imply this recovery. OpenAI constructed a countably infinite family of pairwise nonisomorphic ICC property-$(T)$ groups with isomorphic group factors (OpenAI 2026c, chap. 4, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[OpenAITenAdvances2026](#ref-OpenAITenAdvances2026)]. Independently and concurrently, Zhou constructed another pair (Zhou 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Zhou2026](#ref-Zhou2026)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Arbitrary finite-index factor neighbors

We next allow the second factor to have no given group presentation. A *finite-index factor neighbor* of $M$ is a factor joined to $M$ by a nonzero bifinite correspondence. The arithmetic data describing such a neighbor involve finite-index subgroup isomorphisms rather than an isomorphism of the whole groups.

For a group $A$, a normalized projective unitary representation with multiplier $\beta$ is a map $\sigma:A\to\mathcal U(V)$ satisfying $\sigma(a)\sigma(b)=\beta(a,b)\sigma(ab)$ and $\sigma(e)=I$. We call $\beta$ *finite type* when such a representation exists with $0<\dim_{\mathbb C}V<\infty$; its image need not be finite. For a group homomorphism $\delta$, the pullback cocycle is $\delta^*\omega(a,b)=\omega(\delta(a),\delta(b))$.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-thm-endpoints"></a>
<div id="thm:endpoints" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (All factor endpoints). *Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\mu$ be any normalized scalar cocycle on $\Gamma$, and let $P$ be a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor with separable predual. There is a nonzero bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$P$ correspondence if and only if $$P\cong L_\omega(\Lambda)^t$$ for a countably infinite ICC group $\Lambda$, a normalized scalar cocycle $\omega$ on $\Lambda$, and $t>0$, for which there are finite-index subgroups $A\leq\Gamma$ and $B\leq\Lambda$, an actual isomorphism $\delta:A\to B$, and a nonzero finite-dimensional projective unitary representation of $A$ with multiplier $$\frac{\mu|_{A\times A}}{\delta^*(\omega|_{B\times B})}.$$ In particular, the reconstructed group $\Lambda$ belongs to $\mathscr K$.*

</div>

</div>

The unknown factor $P$ is not assumed to have a group presentation or a preferred basis. Its presentation is recovered from multiplication in a finite-index extension. If $\mu=1$, finite-index projective induction recovers the usual finite-type condition on $\omega$. For arbitrary $\mu$ the ratio condition is essential: $P=L_\mu(\Gamma)$ is always an endpoint.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The arithmetic correspondence category

The two recovery theorems use a common classification of correspondences. We first describe its models and then the finite-dimensional data that control their maps and summands.

<a id="eq:intro-multiplier"></a>
<a id="eq:intro-model"></a>
For finite-index subgroups $A\leq\Gamma$ and $B\leq\Lambda$, an isomorphism $\delta:A\to B$, and a nonzero finite-dimensional projective unitary representation $\sigma$ satisfying $$\label{eq:intro-multiplier}
 \sigma(a)\sigma(b)
 =\frac{\mu(a,b)}{\omega(\delta(a),\delta(b))}\sigma(ab),
 \qquad \sigma(e)=I,$$ define $$\label{eq:intro-model}
 E(A,B,\delta,\sigma)
 =L^2(M)\,\overline\otimes_{L_{\mu|_A}(A)}
       \bigl(V_\sigma\otimes L^2(N)\bigr),
 \qquad M=L_\mu(\Gamma),\quad N=L_\omega(\Lambda).$$ The right $N$ action is standard. The left subgroup action on the second factor is $u_a(\xi\otimes\eta)=\sigma(a)\xi\otimes v_{\delta(a)}\eta$. Equation <a href="#eq:intro-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-multiplier">[eq:intro-multiplier]</a> makes this an action with multiplier $\mu|_A$.

A right action is represented by $\rho(h)\xi=\xi v_h^*$, so $\rho(h)\rho(k)=\overline{\omega(h,k)}\rho(hk)$. The left action is $\pi(g)\xi=u_g\xi$. Together they give the joint action $U_{(g,h)}\xi=u_g\xi v_h^*$.

A *virtual-isomorphism germ* from $\Gamma$ to $\Lambda$ is an isomorphism between finite-index subgroups, modulo agreement on a common finite-index subgroup. The action of $\Gamma\times\Lambda$ on germs is $$(g,h)\cdot[\delta]
   =[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(h)\circ\delta\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(g^{-1})].$$ For a germ $d$ let $C_d$ denote its full stabilizer. ICC implies that $C_d$ is the graph of an isomorphism $A_d\to B_d$ between finite-index subgroups; the proof is included in Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a>. Use the joint multiplier <a id="eq:intro-joint"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-joint}
 \alpha\bigl((g,h),(g',h')\bigr)
 =\mu(g,g')\overline{\omega(h,h')}.$$ For a finite-dimensional $\alpha|_{C_d}$-projective representation $\sigma_d$, define $$H(d,\sigma_d)=E(A_d,B_d,\delta_d,\widetilde\sigma_d),
 \qquad \widetilde\sigma_d(a)=\sigma_d(a,\delta_d(a)).$$ Here $\delta_d:A_d\to B_d$ is the isomorphism whose graph is $C_d$. Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a> realizes $H(d,\sigma_d)$ as the orthogonal sum of copies of the representation space indexed by $(\Gamma\times\Lambda)/C_d$, and describes the induced transports between them.

The full stabilizer matters because a graph presentation can split one of these fibres into several pieces. If $C_\delta=\{(a,\delta(a)):a\in A\}$ represents $d$, the finite set $C_d/C_\delta$ indexes the pieces that must be grouped together. Their projective representation is induced from $C_\delta$ to $C_d$ with multiplier $\alpha$. Bimodule projections may mix these pieces; they become fibre projections only after this grouping.

<a id="stmt-thm-correspondences"></a>
<div id="thm:correspondences" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (All finite correspondences). *Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\Lambda$ be any countably infinite ICC group, and let $\mu$ and $\omega$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles on these groups.*

1.  *Every nonzero bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$L_\omega(\Lambda)$ correspondence is a closed bimodule summand of a finite direct sum of the models in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-model">[eq:intro-model]</a>.*

2.  *Every such correspondence has a finite orthogonal decomposition into full-germ orbit bundles. After choosing distinct germ orbits and allowing zero fibres, all bounded bimodule maps are <a id="eq:intro-hom"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-hom}
     \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{M\text{--}N}\!\left(\bigoplus_d H(d,\sigma_d),
                              \bigoplus_d H(d,\tau_d)\right)
      =\bigoplus_d\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{C_d,\alpha}(\sigma_d,\tau_d).$$ Norm, adjoint, and composition are fibrewise. Projection summands are exactly projective-representation summands in the fibres. A correspondence $K=\bigoplus_d H(d,\sigma_d)$ has dimensions $$\dim_M K=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Lambda:B_d],\qquad
     \dim K_N=\sum_d\dim_{\mathbb C}(\sigma_d)[\Gamma:A_d].$$ Both are positive integers when $K\ne0$.*

3.  *Conjugation and Connes fusion correspond to conjugation and balanced tensor product of these bundles. They are equivalently computed by finite double-coset induction. The scalar transports are given explicitly in Equations <a href="#eq:input-A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A1">[eq:input-A1]</a> and <a href="#eq:input-A2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-A2">[eq:input-A2]</a> and Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a>.*

</div>

The integer dimensions in part <span class="upright">(ii)</span> are the dimensions of the actual correspondence, including a proper summand of a presenting model. That distinction is essential to the stable recovery argument: the two dimensions of an equivalence correspondence are reciprocal, so here both must equal one. The projective data throughout concern only the matched ratio in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-multiplier">[eq:intro-multiplier]</a>; neither individual endpoint cocycle is assumed finite type.

Theorems <a href="#thm:stable" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:stable">2</a> and <a href="#thm:endpoints" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:endpoints">3</a> will follow from this map-level classification in Section <a href="#sec:endpoints" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoints">4</a>. These are structural classifications; they do not assert a decision procedure for arbitrary group presentations or cocycle classes.

For untwisted factors, the same results identify the outer automorphism group and recover the group from its reduced C\*-algebra whenever that algebra has a unique tracial state. Section <a href="#sec:examples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:examples">5</a> also gives commensurability and finite-type neighbor criteria, named lattice examples, and the finite-index endomorphism consequence for $L(\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z))$. The last consequence uses a separate classical covolume argument.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### History and the role of the arithmetic category

Popa’s deformation/rigidity theory produced rigidity theorems for crossed products and Bernoulli actions. Ioana’s $W^*$-superrigidity theorem for Bernoulli actions of ICC property-$(T)$ groups concerns an action together with its crossed product (Ioana 2011) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Ioana2011](#ref-Ioana2011)]. Ioana–Popa–Vaes obtained the first $W^*$-superrigid groups, in classes of generalized wreath products (Ioana, Popa, and Vaes 2013) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[IPV2013](#ref-IPV2013)]. Chifan–Ioana–Osin–Sun constructed the first such groups with property $(T)$, using wreath-like products (Chifan et al. 2023) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CIOS2023](#ref-CIOS2023)]. Rigidity of an action and recovery from a bare group factor are distinct problems, and the correspondence category asks additionally for finite index, multiplicities, and composition.

The geometric route for lattices has its own antecedents. For lattices in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(n,1)/\{\pm1\}$, $n\geq2$, Cowling–Haagerup’s completely bounded approximation invariant already determines $n$ from the group factor (Cowling and Haagerup 1989, Main theorem and Propositions 6.1–6.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CowlingHaagerup1989](#ref-CowlingHaagerup1989)]. Bekka classified the characters of $\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb Z)$ for $n\geq3$, and Peterson proved a finite-factor representation dichotomy for irreducible higher-rank property-$(T)$ lattices (Bekka 2007; Peterson 2014) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Bekka2007](#ref-Bekka2007), [Peterson2014](#ref-Peterson2014)]. Boutonnet–Houdayer developed stationary and noncommutative boundary methods and recovered character rigidity in that framework (Boutonnet and Houdayer 2021) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BoutonnetHoudayer2021](#ref-BoutonnetHoudayer2021)]. These results provide important context for the companion’s geometry. They do not by themselves identify all finite correspondences to arbitrary ICC groups. The companion proves the exhaustion input in the full mixed-local-field class stated here, with reducible products and arbitrary scalar twists.

Jones’s index and the expectation inequality of Pimsner–Popa provide the dimension and positivity framework (Jones 1983; Pimsner and Popa 1986) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Jones1983](#ref-Jones1983), [PimsnerPopa1986](#ref-PimsnerPopa1986)]. We use Connes’s correspondence category and relative tensor product (Connes 1994, chap. V, Appendix B) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Connes1994](#ref-Connes1994)]. Complete finite-index classifications and explicit fusion rules already occur in Vaes’s work on generalized Bernoulli crossed products (Vaes 2008, Theorem 2.2 and Section 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Vaes2008](#ref-Vaes2008)]. Theorem 2.2 allows scalar twists at both endpoints and a finite-dimensional projective representation of their matched ratio; Definition 6.8 and Theorem 6.9 supply projective induction and the cocycle-corrected double-coset rule. We adapt this calculus to graph bundles for twisted group factors. The full stabilizer of a virtual-isomorphism germ identifies every bounded intertwiner and every selecting projection, including projections that mix smaller presenting fibres.

Donvil–Vaes prove rigidity under arbitrary scalar twists and virtual isomorphism for certain left-right wreath products (Donvil and Vaes 2025) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DonvilVaes2025](#ref-DonvilVaes2025)]. Their Proposition 6.5 reconstructs an intermediate factor from finite-dimensional homogeneous spaces and their actual products, and Theorem 6.6 applies this construction to arbitrary bifinite factor neighbors. The source cocycle is already arbitrary there, and the conclusion retains a finite-type ratio of the two cocycles. We adapt their homogeneous-product and minimal-corner argument from automorphism degrees to a grading by virtual automorphism germs of $\Gamma$; finite-index domains and boundedness of the homogeneous vectors are treated explicitly. The companion supplies the distinct exhaustion theorem for the local-field lattice class. The finite-type ascent used for an untwisted source is the projective-induction statement of (Donvil and Vaes 2025, Lemmas 6.7–6.8) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DonvilVaes2025](#ref-DonvilVaes2025)].

Chifan–Fernández Quero–Osin–Tan obtain stable canonical recovery for scalar twists of certain property-$(T)$ wreath-like products when the source cocycle is trivial on the abelian base (Chifan et al. 2026, Theorem F) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CFOT2026](#ref-CFOT2026)]. Their comparison group is arbitrary ICC. The unrestricted source cocycles and lattice class treated here enter through the companion’s distinct exhaustion theorem.

Outside the property-$(T)$ setting, the nonisomorphic free groups $\mathbb F_2$ and $\mathbb F_3$ satisfy $L(\mathbb F_2)\cong L(\mathbb F_3)$ (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[OpenAIFreeGroupFactors2026](#ref-OpenAIFreeGroupFactors2026)]. These factors have fundamental group $\mathbb R_{>0}$ (OpenAI 2026a, Corollary 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[OpenAIFreeGroupFactors2026](#ref-OpenAIFreeGroupFactors2026)], in contrast to the trivial fundamental groups in Theorem <a href="#thm:stable" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:stable">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Proof strategy and navigation

There are three algebraic steps. First, the graph models admit orthogonal finite-dimensional fibres indexed by graph cosets. Fibres with the same virtual-isomorphism germ must be grouped together before passing to a summand. The full stabilizer of a germ has only one finite orbit on the germ space. Square summability of each column of a bounded intertwiner therefore forces that map to preserve each grouped fibre. This proves the Hom formula and makes the two dimensions sums of integer fibre ranks times subgroup indices.

Second, a bifinite correspondence with an unknown factor produces a finite-index extension of the known group factor. Arithmetic exhaustion grades its $L^2$ space by finite-dimensional germ fibres. The Pimsner–Popa inequality turns those vectors into bounded operators. Products can then be computed on a common subgroup domain, and they multiply the germ degrees. In a minimal corner of the identity fibre, every nonzero degree is a unitary line. Those lines, with their actual products and trace, recover the group and scalar cocycle. The identity block supplies the projective representation of the cocycle ratio.

Third, the equivalence correspondence of a specified stable isomorphism has reciprocal dimensions. Their integrality forces both to be one, leaving one full graph and one one-dimensional projective fibre. The correspondence identification recovers the cochain. The column realizations in the given matrix corners then recover the implementing partial isometry for the specified map. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map">1</a> separates these two consequences of the category calculation.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>Arithmetic exhaustion from the companion supplies the geometric input for the main classification. The model calculus is independent of that input. Actual multiplication and reciprocal dimensions are separate final arguments.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a> records the relative tensor convention and exact companion interface. Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a> proves all model formulas, then applies exhaustion. Section <a href="#sec:endpoints" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoints">4</a> reconstructs factor neighbors and specified stable maps. Section <a href="#sec:examples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:examples">5</a> proves the further consequences described above.

<a id="sec:setup"></a>
### Finite modules and the exhaustion interface

Our algebraic calculations need only countability and ICC. The geometric class enters at one precise point: the exhaustion statement below says that the explicit models contain every bifinite correspondence. We first fix the norm convention for their construction.

For completeness, $L_\mu(\Gamma)$ is a factor when $\Gamma$ is ICC: the absolute values of the Fourier coefficients of a central $L^2$ vector are constant on conjugacy classes, so square summability kills every nonidentity coefficient.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Relative tensor products and elementary dimensions

All Hilbert-space inner products are linear in the second variable. A vector $\xi$ in an $M$–$N$ correspondence is *right bounded* if $R_\xi\widehat a=\xi a$ extends to a bounded map from $L^2(N)$. For an $N$–$P$ correspondence, the relative tensor product is the completion, after quotienting out zero-norm vectors, of the tensors with right-bounded first vector and inner product <a id="eq:relative-inner-product"></a>$$\label{eq:relative-inner-product}
 \langle\xi\otimes_N\eta,\xi'\otimes_N\eta'\rangle
 =\langle\eta,(R_\xi^*R_{\xi'})\eta'\rangle.$$ The right-module map $R_\xi^*R_{\xi'}$ is left multiplication by an element of $N$. Balancing means $(\xi a)\otimes_N\eta=\xi\otimes_N(a\eta)$ for $a\in N$. This is the finite tracial form of Connes’s relative tensor product (Connes 1994, chap. V, Appendix B, Theorems 13 and 20) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Connes1994](#ref-Connes1994)].

A finite right $N$-module has a column realization $pL^2(N)^n$, with dimension $(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_n\otimes\tau_N)(p)$. The vectors in $pN^n$ are right bounded and dense. The projection model and its dimension are independent of the chosen realization (Jones 2009, Theorem 10.1.1 and Proposition 10.1.3) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Jones2009](#ref-Jones2009)]. We prove the normality of the graph models directly in Lemmas <a href="#lem:calculus-induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:calculus-induction">7</a> and <a href="#lem:calculus-graph-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:calculus-graph-model">8</a>. The same elementary construction precedes the geometric argument in the companion, so its exhaustion proof has no dependence on our all-maps or summand classification. For $n=\dim\sigma$, the elementary dimensions are $n[\Lambda:B]$ and $n[\Gamma:A]$ in left/right order. The dimensions of *actual summands* will require the full-stabilizer calculation.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### The geometric input

The real simple factors in Definition <a href="#def:class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:class">1</a> have higher rank or are locally $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(n,1)$, $n\geq2$, or $F_{4(-20)}$. At a finite place their relative rank is at least two. Complex places are treated as real Lie groups in differential arguments. Property $(T)$ passes between a locally compact group and a lattice and between a discrete group and a finite-index subgroup; see (Bekka, Harpe, and Valette 2008) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[BHV2008](#ref-BHV2008)]. In particular, that class is closed under abstract commensurability among countably infinite ICC groups. The geometric companion treats arbitrary lattices in the permitted finite products, without a dense-projection hypothesis. We use exactly the following theorem.

<a id="stmt-prop-recovery-exhaustion"></a>
<div id="prop:recovery-exhaustion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Arithmetic exhaustion, companion Theorem 1.2). *Let $\Gamma\in\mathscr K$, let $\Lambda$ be any countably infinite ICC group, and let $\mu,\omega$ be arbitrary normalized scalar cocycles. Every nonzero separable bifinite $L_\mu(\Gamma)$–$L_\omega(\Lambda)$ correspondence is a closed bimodule summand of a finite direct sum of the models $E(A,B,\delta,\sigma)$ in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-model">[eq:intro-model]</a>, with the exact multiplier in Equation <a href="#eq:intro-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-multiplier">[eq:intro-multiplier]</a>.*

</div>

This is the Arithmetic exhaustion theorem of the complete companion (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ArithmeticCompanion](#ref-ArithmeticCompanion)]. Its conclusion retains actual finite-index subgroup isomorphisms and their exact scalar ratios. We do not assume that the projective representations have finite image, that either cocycle is finite type, or that the original correspondence is irreducible. The algebraic proofs that follow are independent of this proposition until its explicit application at the end of Section <a href="#sec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:calculus">3</a>.

<a id="sec:calculus"></a>
### Full-germ correspondence calculus

The calculations in this section are algebraic. We give a group-factor version of the projective induction and cocycle-corrected double-coset calculus in Vaes’s work on generalized Bernoulli crossed products (Vaes 2008, Theorem 2.2 and Section 6.2, especially Definition 6.8 and Theorem 6.9) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Vaes2008](#ref-Vaes2008)]. We prove the formulas here, including the full-stabilizer argument for every bounded intertwiner and every bimodule summand. Until we apply Proposition <a href="#prop:recovery-exhaustion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:recovery-exhaustion">5</a>, the groups need only be countable and ICC; the geometric hypotheses of Definition <a href="#def:class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:class">1</a> are not used. We first construct the arithmetic models with their exact scalar transports, and then determine their intertwiners, summands, conjugates, and relative tensor products. This order also verifies that every model appearing in the exhaustion is a normal bifinite correspondence.

Write $$M=L_\mu(\Gamma),\qquad N=L_\omega(\Lambda),\qquad
 D=\Gamma\times\Lambda,
